N^{5}\geq\max\{n_{0}(N),n_{1}(N)\}
. If this is not true, we may replace
N^{5},N^{6}
by
N^{b},N^{b+1}
respectively, where
b
is any integer satisfying
N^{b}\geq\max\{n_{0}(N),n_{1}(N)\}
.
respectively, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\overline{C_{N}^{2}(\xi)})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\overline{\mathcal{E}_{N}})+\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\overline{C_{N}^{2}(\xi)}\cap\mathcal{E}_{N})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\frac{1}{N^{2}}+\sum_{n=N^{5}}^{N^{6}}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}^{\mathcal{E}_{N}}(\|\widehat{\bm{\eta}}(n)-\bm{\eta}\|>\xi)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}\frac{1}{N^{2}}+\sum_{n=N^{5}}^{N^{6}}\frac{2^{K/2}\,n^{3K/8}}{c_{N}^{K/2}}\ \exp\left(-\frac{c_{N}\,\xi^{2}\,n^{1/4}}{2(2\,M_{f})}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{\leq}}\frac{1}{N^{2}}+\frac{2^{K/2+2}\,K^{K/4}}{\sigma_{\bm{\theta}}^{K/4}}\,\sum_{n=N^{5}}^{N^{6}}n^{3K/8}\,N\,\exp\left(-\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{\bm{\theta}}}\,\xi^{2}\,n^{1/4}}{8\,\sqrt{K}\,(2\,M_{f})\,N}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(d)}}{{\leq}}\frac{1}{N^{2}}+\frac{2^{K/2+2}\,K^{K/4}}{\sigma_{\bm{\theta}}^{K/4}}\,N^{9K/4+1}\,\exp\left(-\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{\bm{\theta}}}\,\xi^{2}\,N^{1/4}}{8\,\sqrt{K}\,(2\,M_{f})}\right)(N^{6}-N^{5}+1)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N^{2}}+\frac{2^{K/2+2}\,K^{K/4}}{\sigma_{\bm{\theta}}^{K/4}}\,N^{9K/4+7}\,\exp\left(-\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{\bm{\theta}}}\,\xi^{2}\,N^{1/4}}{8\,\sqrt{K}\,(2\,M_{f})}\right),

(141)

where

•

(a)
follows from noting that
\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\overline{\mathcal{E}_{N}})\leq 1/N^{2}
(applying Lemma
6.1
with
\alpha=1/N^{2}
).

•

(b)
follows from (
140
) (with
c
and
n_{1}
replaced by
c_{N}
and
n_{1}(N)
respectively).

•

(c)
follows by noting that

\displaystyle c_{N}

\displaystyle=\frac{S_{R}}{2\,\lambda_{\bm{\theta}}(N)^{1/4}}

\displaystyle=\frac{S_{R}\sqrt{\sigma_{\bm{\theta}}}}{2\,\sqrt{1+S_{R}}\,\sqrt{\log(1+KS_{R}\,N^{2})}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{S_{R}\sqrt{\sigma_{\bm{\theta}}}}{2\,\sqrt{1+S_{R}}\,\sqrt{KS_{R}\,N^{2}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{S_{R}\,\sigma_{\bm{\theta}}}}{2\,N\,\sqrt{K(1+S_{R})}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{\bm{\theta}}}}{2\,N\sqrt{2K}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{\bm{\theta}}}}{4\,N\,\sqrt{K}},

where the penultimate line above follows from using
1+S_{R}\leq 2\,S_{R}
.

•

(d)
follows by noting that
n^{3K/8}<(N^{6})^{3K/8}=N^{9K/4}
and
n^{1/4}\geq N^{5/4}
for
n\in\{N^{5},\ldots,N^{6}\}
.

This completes the proof.
∎

Appendix I
Proof of Lemma
6.3

Before we begin the proof, we note the following results of interest.

Lemma I.1
.

[
41
, Theorem 2]

Let
Q_{1}
(resp.
Q_{2}
) be the transition kernel of a Markov chain with stationary distribution
\omega_{1}
(resp.
\omega_{2}
). Let
Z_{1}\coloneqq(I-Q_{1}+\mathbf{1}\omega_{1}^{\top})^{-1}
, where
I
is the identity matrix (of the same dimension as
Q_{1}
and
Q_{2}
), and
\mathbf{1}
denotes the all-ones vector. Then,

\omega_{2}^{\top}-\omega_{1}^{\top}=\omega_{2}^{\top}[Q_{2}-Q_{1}]\,Z_{1},\quad\|\omega_{2}-\omega_{1}\|_{1}\leq\|Z_{1}\|_{\infty}\,\|Q_{2}-Q_{1}\|_{\infty}.

(142)

Lemma I.2
.

[
32
, Lemma 23]

Let
Q_{\bm{\theta,\pi}}
be the transition kernel of MDP
\mathcal{M}_{\bm{\theta},R}
under policy
\pi
. Let
\omega_{\bm{\theta},\pi}
denote the stationary distribution of
Q_{\bm{\theta,\pi}}
, and let

\|Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi}^{n}-\mathbf{1}\omega_{\bm{\theta},\pi}^{\top}\|_{\infty}\leq C_{\pi}\,\rho_{\pi}^{n}\quad\forall n\in\mathbb{N}.

(143)

Then, for any bounded function
g
on
\mathcal{Z}=\mathbb{S}_{R}\times[K]
, the function

\widehat{g}_{\pi}(z)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\ \sum_{z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}}Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi}^{n}(z^{\prime}|z)\,\bigg(g(z^{\prime})-\langle\omega_{\bm{\theta},\pi},g\rangle\bigg),\quad z\in\mathcal{Z},

(144)

is well-defined and satisfies the so-called Poisson equation

\widehat{g}_{\pi}(z)-\sum_{z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}}Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi}(z^{\prime}|z)\,\widehat{g}_{\pi}(z^{\prime})=g(z)-\langle\omega_{\bm{\theta},\pi},g\rangle\quad\forall z\in\mathcal{Z}.

(145)

Furthermore,

\|\widehat{g}_{\pi}\|_{\infty}\leq L_{\pi}\,\|g\|_{\infty},

(146)

where
L_{\pi}\coloneqq\dfrac{C_{\pi}}{1-\rho_{\pi}}
, and for any pair of policies
\pi,\pi^{\prime}
,

\max_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}\bigg\lvert\sum_{z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}}Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi}(z^{\prime}|z)\,\widehat{g}_{\pi}(z^{\prime})-\sum_{z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}}Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi^{\prime}}(z^{\prime}|z)\,\widehat{g}_{\pi^{\prime}}(z^{\prime})\bigg\rvert\leq L_{\pi}\,\|g\|_{\infty}\bigg[\|\omega_{\bm{\theta},\pi}-\omega_{\bm{\theta},\pi^{\prime}}\|_{1}+L_{\pi}\,\|Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi}-Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi^{\prime}}\|_{\infty}\bigg].

(147)

Proof of Lemma
6.3
.

We now begin the proof of Lemma
6.3
. Let us fix
z=(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in\mathbb{S}_{R}\times[K]
. Let

\displaystyle D_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{n}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{N(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)}{n-K+1}-\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n-K+1}\sum_{t=K}^{n}\mathbf{1}_{\{\mathbf{d}(t)=\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i}(t)=\mathbf{i},A_{t}=a\}}-\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum\limits_{t=K}^{\sqrt{N}}[\mathbf{1}_{\{\mathbf{d}(t)=\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i}(t)=\mathbf{i},A_{t}=a\}}-\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)]}{n-K+1}+\frac{\sum\limits_{t=\sqrt{N}+1}^{n}[\mathbf{1}_{\{\mathbf{d}(t)=\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i}(t)=\mathbf{i},A_{t}=a\}}-\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)]}{n-K+1}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{\sum\limits_{t=K}^{\sqrt{N}}[\mathbf{1}_{\{\mathbf{d}(t)=\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i}(t)=\mathbf{i},A_{t}=a\}}-\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)]}{n-K+1}}_{D_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{n}(1)}+\underbrace{\frac{\sum\limits_{t=\sqrt{N}+1}^{n}[\mathbf{1}_{\{\mathbf{d}(t)=\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i}(t)=\mathbf{i},A_{t}=a\}}-\omega_{\bm{\theta},t-1}^{\star}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)]}{n-K+1}}_{D_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{n}(2)}

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt+\underbrace{\frac{\sum\limits_{t=\sqrt{N}+1}^{n}[\omega_{\bm{\theta},t-1}^{\star}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)-\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)]}{n-K+1}}_{D_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{n}(3)},

(148)

where for each
t\geq\sqrt{N}+1
, the quantity
\omega_{\bm{\theta},t-1}^{\star}
denotes the stationary distribution of the kernel
Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi}
under the policy
\pi=\pi_{t-1}=\varepsilon_{t-1}\,\pi^{\text{\rm unif}}+(1-\varepsilon_{t-1})\,\pi_{\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(t-2)}^{\eta}
obtained by using a fixed
\nu_{t-2}^{\star}\in\mathcal{W}^{\star}(\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(t-2))
in (
34
)); such a policy is indeed ergodic thanks to Lemma
3.3
. We now upper bound each of the terms in (
148
) individually. First, note that for all
n\geq N^{3/4}
and for all
N\geq(1/\xi)^{4}
, we have

\displaystyle|D_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{n}(1)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{N}-K+1}{n-K+1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{N}}{n}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}

\displaystyle\leq\xi.

(149)

Next, we note using Lemma
I.1
that

\displaystyle|D_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{n}(3)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sum\limits_{t=\sqrt{N}+1}^{n}\bigg\lvert\omega_{\bm{\theta},t-1}^{\star}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)-\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\bigg\rvert}{n-K+1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sum\limits_{t=\sqrt{N}+1}^{n}\|\omega_{\bm{\theta},t-1}^{\star}-\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star}\|_{1}}{n-K+1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\kappa_{\bm{\theta},\nu}\,\frac{\sum\limits_{t=\sqrt{N}+1}^{n}\|Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{t-1}}-Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{\bm{\theta}}^{\eta}(\nu)}\|_{\infty}}{n-K+1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}\kappa_{\bm{\theta},\nu}\,\frac{\sum\limits_{t=\sqrt{N}+1}^{n}\|\varepsilon_{t-1}\,Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi^{\text{\rm unif}}}+(1-\varepsilon_{t-1})\,Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(t-2)}^{\eta}}-Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{\bm{\theta}}^{\eta}(\nu)}\|_{\infty}}{n-K+1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{\leq}}\kappa_{\bm{\theta},\nu}\,\frac{\sum\limits_{t=\sqrt{N}+1}^{n}\varepsilon_{t-1}+\|Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(t-2)}^{\eta}}-Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{\bm{\theta}}^{\eta}(\nu)}\|_{\infty}}{n-K+1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(d)}}{{\leq}}\kappa_{\bm{\theta},\nu}\,\frac{\sum\limits_{t=\sqrt{N}+1}^{n}\varepsilon_{t-1}+\|\pi_{\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(t-2)}^{\eta}-\pi_{\bm{\theta}}^{\eta}(\nu)\|_{\infty}}{n-K+1},

(150)

where
\kappa_{\bm{\theta},\nu}=\|(I+Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{\bm{\theta}}^{\eta}(\nu)}+\mathbf{1}\,(\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star})^{\top})^{-1}\|_{\infty}
,
(a)
above follows from Lemma
6.3
with
\pi_{\bm{\theta}}^{\eta}(\nu)
denoting the quantity in (
34
) with (i)
\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(n)
replaced by
\bm{\theta}
, and (ii)
\nu_{n}^{\star}
replaced by
\nu
,
(b)
above follows from the definition of the transition kernel
Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{t-1}}
,
(c)
above follows from the observation that

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{t-1}\,Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi^{\text{\rm unif}}}+(1-\varepsilon_{t-1})\,Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(t-2)}^{\eta}}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon_{t-1}+Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(t-2)}^{\eta}},

(151)

and
(d)
above follows from the observation that

\displaystyle\|Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(t-2)}^{\eta}}-Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{\bm{\theta}}^{\eta}(\nu)}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\|Q_{\bm{\theta},R}\,[\pi_{\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(t-2)}^{\eta}-\pi_{\bm{\theta}}^{\eta}(\nu)]\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|\pi_{\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(t-2)}^{\eta}-\pi_{\bm{\theta}}^{\eta}(\nu)\|_{\infty},

where the last line above is an artefact of
\|Q_{\bm{\theta},R}\|\leq 1
. Thanks to Lemma
6.1
, we have
\bm{\theta}(n)\to\bm{\theta}
almost surely as
n\to\infty
. We then note that by virtue of the upper-hemicontinuity of the map
\bm{\lambda}\to\mathcal{W}^{\star}(\bm{\lambda})
, there exists
N_{1}=N_{1}(\xi)>0
and
\rho(\xi)>0
such that for all
N\geq N_{1}
and for all
t\geq\sqrt{N}+1
, we have

\displaystyle\|\pi_{\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(t-2)}^{\eta}-\pi_{\bm{\theta}}^{\eta}(\nu)\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\bigg\|\frac{\eta\,\nu_{\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(t-2)}^{\text{\rm unif}}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)+(1-\eta)\,\nu_{t-2}^{\star}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)}{\eta\,\mu_{\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(t-2)}^{\text{\rm unif}}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i})+(1-\eta)\,\sum_{a^{\prime}=1}^{K}\nu_{t-2}^{\star}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a^{\prime})}-\frac{\eta\,\nu_{{\bm{\theta}}}^{\text{\rm unif}}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)+(1-\eta)\,\nu(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)}{\eta\,\mu_{{\bm{\theta}}}^{\text{\rm unif}}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i})+(1-\eta)\,\sum_{a^{\prime}=1}^{K}\nu(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a^{\prime})}\bigg\|

\displaystyle\leq\rho(\xi).

(152)

Using (
152
) in (
150
), we get

|D_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{n}(3)|\leq\kappa_{\bm{\theta},\nu}\,\rho(\xi)\quad\forall n\geq\sqrt{N}+1,~\forall N\geq N_{1}(\xi).

(153)

Lastly, in order to bound
D_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{n}(2)
, we use the function
\widehat{g}_{t-1}=\widehat{g}_{\pi_{t-1}}
, the solution to the Poisson equation in (
145
) with
g(z)=\mathbf{1}_{\{(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\}}(z)
. From Lemma
I.2
, we know that
\widehat{g}_{t-1}
exists, and using the shorthand notation
z_{t}=(\mathbf{d}(t),\mathbf{i}(t),A_{t})
, we may express
D_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{n}(2)
as

\displaystyle D_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{n}(2)

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum\limits_{t=\sqrt{N}+1}^{n}[\widehat{g}_{t-1}(z_{t})-Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{t-1}}\,\widehat{g}_{t-1}(z_{t})]}{n-K+1}

\displaystyle=D_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{n}(2,1)+D_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{n}(2,2)+D_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{n}(2,3),

(154)

where

\displaystyle D_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{n}(2,1)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{\sum\limits_{t=\sqrt{N}+1}^{n}[\widehat{g}_{t-1}(z_{t})-Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{t-1}}\,\widehat{g}_{t-1}(z_{t-1})]}{n-K+1},

(155)

\displaystyle D_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{n}(2,2)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{\sum\limits_{t=\sqrt{N}+1}^{n}[Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{t}}\,\widehat{g}_{t}(z_{t})-Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{t-1}}\,\widehat{g}_{t-1}(z_{t})]}{n-K+1},

(156)

\displaystyle D_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{n}(2,3)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\dfrac{Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{\sqrt{N}}}\,\widehat{g}_{\sqrt{N}}(z_{\sqrt{N}})-Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{n}}\,\widehat{g}_{n}(z_{n})}{n-K+1},

(157)

Bounding
D_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{n}(2,1)
:
To bound (
155
), we note that
G_{n}=(n-K+1)\,D_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{n}(2,1)
is a martingale. Furthermore, by Lemma
I.2
, we have

\displaystyle|G_{n}-G_{n-1}|

\displaystyle=\bigg\lvert\widehat{g}_{n-1}(z_{n})-Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{n-1}}\,\widehat{g}_{n-1}(z_{n-1})\bigg\rvert

\displaystyle\leq 2\,\|\widehat{g}_{n-1}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq 2\,\frac{C_{n-1}}{1-\rho_{n-1}},

(158)

where
C_{n-1}
and
\rho_{n-1}
are constants as defined in Lemma
M.2
. For all for all
n\geq\sqrt{N}+1
and for all
N\geq\max\{N_{1}(\xi)+1,(1/\xi)^{4(1+S_{R})}\}
, where
N_{1}(\xi)
is as defined above, we note that

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{n-1}=(n-1)^{-\frac{1}{2(1+S_{R})}}\leq N^{-\frac{1}{4(1+S_{R})}}\leq\xi,

(159)

\displaystyle\|\pi_{\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(n-2)}^{\eta}-\pi_{\bm{\theta}}^{\eta}(\nu)\|_{\infty}\leq\rho(\xi),

(160)

\displaystyle\|\omega_{\bm{\theta},n-1}^{\star}-\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star}\|_{1}\leq\varepsilon_{n-1}+\|\pi_{\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(n-2)}^{\eta}-\pi_{\bm{\theta}}^{\eta}(\nu)\|_{\infty}\|\leq 2\,\xi.

(161)

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\frac{C_{n-1}}{1-\rho_{n-1}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2/\bar{\sigma}(\varepsilon_{n-1},\pi_{\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(n-2)}^{\eta},\omega_{\bm{\theta},n-1}^{\star})}{1-\bar{\sigma}(\varepsilon_{n-1},\pi_{\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(n-2)}^{\eta},\omega_{\bm{\theta},n-1}^{\star})}

\displaystyle\leq L_{\xi}\coloneqq\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varepsilon:|\varepsilon|\leq\xi\\
\pi:\|\pi-\pi_{\bm{\theta}}^{\eta}(\nu)\|_{\infty}\leq\rho(\xi)\\
\omega:\|\omega-\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star}\|_{1}\leq 2\,\xi\end{subarray}}\frac{2/\bar{\sigma}(\varepsilon,\pi,\omega)}{1-\bar{\sigma}(\varepsilon,\pi,\omega)}.

(162)

In (
162
),
\bar{\sigma}
is as defined in (
215
). Using (
158
), (
162
), and the Azuma-Hoeffding inequality for bounded martingale sequences, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}\bigg(|D_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{n}(2,1)|\geq 2\,L_{\xi}\,\xi\bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}\bigg(|G_{n}|\geq(n-K+1)\,2\,L_{\xi}\,\xi\bigg)

\displaystyle=O\bigg(\exp(-n\,\xi^{2})\bigg)\quad\forall n\geq\sqrt{N}+1,~\forall N\geq\max\{N_{1}(\xi),(1/\xi)^{4(1+S_{R})}\}.

(163)

Bounding
D_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{n}(2,2)
:
Writing
L_{t}=C_{t}/(1-\rho_{t})
for all
t
, we note from Lemma
I.2
that

\displaystyle|D_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{n}(2,2)|

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum\limits_{t=\sqrt{N}+1}^{n}L_{t}\bigg[\|\omega_{\bm{\theta},t}^{\star}-\omega_{\bm{\theta},t-1}^{\star}\|_{1}+L_{t-1}\,\|Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{t}}-Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{t-1}}\|_{\infty}\bigg]}{n-K+1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sum\limits_{t=\sqrt{N}+1}^{n}L_{\xi}\bigg[\|\omega_{\bm{\theta},t}^{\star}-\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star}\|_{1}+\|\omega_{\bm{\theta},t-1}^{\star}-\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star}\|_{1}+L_{\xi}\,\|Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{t}}-Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{t-1}}\|_{\infty}\bigg]}{n-K+1}.

(164)

We then note that

\displaystyle\|Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{t}}-Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{t-1}}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{t}}-Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{\bm{\theta}}^{\eta}(\nu)}\|+\|Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{t-1}}-Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{\bm{\theta}}^{\eta}(\nu)}\|

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon_{t-1}+\varepsilon_{t}+\|\pi_{\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(t-1)}^{\eta}-\pi_{\bm{\theta}}^{\eta}(\nu)\|_{\infty}+\|\pi_{\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(t-2)}^{\eta}-\pi_{\bm{\theta}}^{\eta}(\nu)\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq 2\,\varepsilon_{t}+2\,\xi\quad\forall t\geq N_{1}(\xi)+1

\displaystyle\leq 2\,t^{-\frac{1}{2(1+S_{R})}}+2\,\xi\quad\forall t\geq N_{1}(\xi)+1

\displaystyle\leq 4\xi\quad\forall t\geq\max\{N_{1}(\xi)+1,(1/\xi)^{2(1+S_{R})}\},

(165)

where the second line follows from (
151
), and the third line follows from using
\varepsilon_{t-1}>\varepsilon_{t}
. Plugging (
165
) in (
164
), we get

\displaystyle|D_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{n}(2,2)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sum\limits_{t=\sqrt{N}+1}^{n}L_{\xi}\bigg[\|\omega_{\bm{\theta},t}^{\star}-\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star}\|_{1}+\|\omega_{\bm{\theta},t-1}^{\star}-\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star}\|_{1}+4\,L_{\xi}\,\xi\bigg]}{n-K+1}

\displaystyle\leq L_{\xi}\bigg[2\,\kappa_{\bm{\theta},\nu}\,\rho(\xi)+4\,L_{\xi}\,\xi\bigg],

(166)

where the last line above follows from using the chain of inequalities in (
150
) along with (
151
) and (
152
).

Bounding
D_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{n}(2,3)
:
To bound (
157
), we note that

\displaystyle|D_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{n}(2,3)|

\displaystyle\leq\bigg\lvert\dfrac{Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{\sqrt{N}}}\,\widehat{g}_{\sqrt{N}}(z_{\sqrt{N}})-Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{n}}\,\widehat{g}_{n}(z_{n})}{n-K+1}\bigg\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{\|\widehat{g}_{\sqrt{N}}\|_{\infty}+\|\widehat{g}_{\sqrt{n}}\|_{\infty}}{n-K+1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{L_{\sqrt{N}}+L_{\sqrt{n}}}{n-K+1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\,L_{\xi}}{n-K+1}

\displaystyle\leq 2\,L_{\xi}\,\xi\quad\forall n\geq 1/\xi+K-1,

(167)

where the second line above follows from using
\|Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{\sqrt{N}}}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
,
\|Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{\sqrt{n}}}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
, and the second line above follows from Lemma
I.2
.

Combining all of the results above, we get for every
(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in\mathbb{S}_{R}\times[K]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}\left(\left\lvert\frac{N(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)}{n-K+1}-\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\right\rvert>K_{\xi}(\bm{\theta},\nu)\,\xi\right)=O\bigg(\exp\left(-n\xi^{2}\right)\bigg),

(168)

where
K_{\xi}(\bm{\theta},\nu)\coloneqq 1+\kappa_{\bm{\theta},\nu}\,\frac{\rho(\xi)}{\xi}+4\,L_{\xi}+L_{\xi}\bigg[2\,\kappa_{\bm{\theta},\nu}\,\frac{\rho(\xi)}{\xi}+4\,L_{\xi}\bigg]
. Noting that

\lim_{\xi\downarrow 0}L_{\xi}=\frac{2/\bar{\sigma}(0,\pi_{\bm{\theta}}(\nu),\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star})}{1-\bar{\sigma}(0,\pi_{\bm{\theta}}(\nu),\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star})}<+\infty,\quad\lim_{\xi\downarrow 0}\frac{\rho(\xi)}{\xi}=0,

we get
\limsup_{\xi\downarrow 0}K_{\xi}(\bm{\theta},\nu)<+\infty
. The final result in (
46
) follows from (
168
) via union bound.
∎

Appendix J
Proof of Proposition
6.4

Our proof is inspired by the proof of Lemma 15 in
[
32
]
, which in turn is inspired by the proof of Proposition 1 in
[
42
]
that is based on the idea of constructing mixture martingales. Note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\tau<\infty,\ \eta_{\widehat{a}}<\eta_{a^{\star}(\bm{\theta})})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\exists n\geq K:\tau_{\pi}=n,\ \eta_{\widehat{a}}<\eta_{a^{\star}(\bm{\theta})})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\exists n\geq K:Z(n)\geq\zeta(n,\delta),\ \eta_{\widehat{a}}<\eta_{a^{\star}(\bm{\theta})})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}\left(\exists n\geq K:\inf_{\bm{\lambda}\in\textsc{Alt}(\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(n)}\sum_{(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i})\in\mathbb{S}_{R}}\ \sum_{a=1}^{K}N(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\,D_{\text{\rm KL}}(\widehat{Q}_{n}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\|Q_{\bm{\lambda},R}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a))\geq\zeta(n,\delta),\ \eta_{\widehat{a}}<\eta_{a^{\star}(\bm{\theta})}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}\left(\exists n\geq K:\sum_{(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i})\in\mathbb{S}_{R}}\ \sum_{a=1}^{K}N(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\,D_{\text{\rm KL}}(\widehat{Q}_{n}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\|Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a))\geq\zeta(n,\delta),\ \eta_{\widehat{a}}<\eta_{a^{\star}(\bm{\theta})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}\left(\exists n\geq K:\sum_{(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i})\in\mathbb{S}_{R}}\ \sum_{a=1}^{K}N(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\,D_{\text{\rm KL}}(\widehat{Q}_{n}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\|Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a))\geq\zeta(n,\delta)\right),

(169)

where
(a)
above follows by noting that
\bm{\theta}\in\textsc{Alt}(\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(n))
under the event
\{\eta_{\widehat{a}}<\eta_{a^{\star}(\bm{\theta})}\}
. Below, we construct a martingale
\{M_{n}\}_{n=K}^{\infty}
such that for all
n\geq K
,

\displaystyle M_{n}

\displaystyle\geq\exp\bigg(\sum_{(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i})\in\mathbb{S}_{R}}\ \sum_{a=1}^{K}N(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\,D_{\text{\rm KL}}(\widehat{Q}_{n}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\|Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a))

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt-(S_{R}-1)\,\sum_{(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i})\in\mathbb{S}_{R}}\ \sum_{a=1}^{K}\,\log\left(e\left[1+\frac{N(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)}{S_{R}-1}\right]\right)\bigg)\quad\text{almost surely}.

(170)

Then, using Doob’s maximal inequality, we argue that

\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\exists n\geq K:M_{n}>1/\delta)\leq\delta,

(171)

which straightforwardly proves the desired result.

Preliminary notations:

We start with some notations. Given
\bm{\gamma}\in\mathbb{R}^{S_{R}-1}
and a probability distribution
\bm{p}=(p_{1},\ldots,p_{S_{R}})
on
\mathbb{S}_{R}
, let

\langle\bm{\gamma},\bm{p}\rangle\coloneqq p_{S_{R}}+\sum_{s=1}^{S_{R}-1}p_{s}\,\lambda_{s},\quad\phi_{\bm{p}}(\bm{\gamma})\coloneqq\log\left(p_{S_{R}}+\sum_{s=1}^{S_{R}-1}p_{s}\,e^{\lambda_{s}}\right).

(172)

Given
(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in\mathbb{S}_{R}
, let
\phi_{\bm{\theta},\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a}(\bm{\gamma})=\phi_{Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)}(\bm{\gamma})
. Define
N(K-1,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)=0
for all
(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in\mathbb{S}_{R}\times[K]
.

Construction of a martingale for each state-action:

Fix
(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in\mathbb{S}_{R}\times[K]
and
\bm{\gamma}\in\mathbb{R}^{S_{R}-1}
. Let

M_{n}^{\bm{\gamma}}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\coloneqq\exp\left(N(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\,\left[\langle\bm{\gamma},\widehat{Q}_{n}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\rangle-\phi_{\bm{\theta},\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a}(\bm{\gamma})\right]\right),\quad n\geq K-1,

(173)

where
\widehat{Q}_{n}
is as defined in (
37
). Then,
\{M_{n}^{\bm{\gamma}}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\}_{n=K-1}^{\infty}
is a martingale with respect to the filtration defined in (
9
). Indeed, using the shorthand notation
z_{n}=(\mathbf{d}(n),\mathbf{i}(n),A_{n})
, we note that almost surely,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}}[M_{n}^{\bm{\gamma}}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)|\mathcal{F}_{n-1},z_{n}=(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)]

(174)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}}\left[\exp\left(N(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\,\left[\langle\bm{\gamma},\widehat{Q}_{n}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\rangle-\phi_{\bm{\theta},\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a}(\bm{\gamma})\right]\right)\bigg|\mathcal{F}_{n-1},z_{n}=(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{X}\sim Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)}\bigg[\exp\bigg((N(n-1,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)+1)\,\bigg[\left\langle\bm{\gamma},\frac{N(n-1,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\,\widehat{Q}_{n-1}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)+\bm{X}}{N(n-1,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)+1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 341.43306pt-\phi_{\bm{\theta},\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a}(\bm{\gamma})\bigg]\bigg)\bigg|\mathcal{F}_{n-1}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{X}\sim Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)}\left[M_{n-1}^{\bm{\gamma}}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\exp\left(\langle\bm{\gamma},\bm{X}\rangle-\phi_{\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a}(\bm{\gamma})\right)\bigg|\mathcal{F}_{n-1}\right]

\displaystyle=M_{n-1}^{\bm{\gamma}}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a).

(175)

In the above set of equalities,
\bm{X}=[\mathbf{1}_{\{\mathbf{d}(n+1)=\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}(n+1)=\mathbf{i}^{\prime}\}}:(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{S}_{R}]^{\top}
, and the last line follows by noting that

\log\mathbb{E}_{\bm{X}\sim Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)}[\exp(\langle\bm{\gamma},\bm{X}\rangle)]=\phi_{\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a}(\bm{\gamma}).

(176)

When
z_{n}\neq(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)
, we have
N(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)=N(n-1,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)
and
\widehat{Q}_{n}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)=\widehat{Q}_{n-1}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)
, and therefore
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}}[M_{n}^{\bm{\gamma}}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)M_{n}\mid\mathcal{F}_{n-1},z_{n}\neq(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)]=M_{n-1}^{\bm{\gamma}}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)
holds trivially.

Construction of a mixture martingale:

For each
(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in\mathbb{S}_{R}\times[K]
, we now construct a
mixture
of the martingales
\{M_{n}^{\bm{\gamma}}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\}_{n=K-1}^{\infty}
as follows. For any
\bm{\gamma}\in\mathbb{R}^{S_{R}-1}
and probability distribution
\bm{p}
on
\mathbb{S}_{R}
, let
\{\bm{p}^{\bm{\gamma}}:\bm{\gamma}\in\mathbb{R}^{S_{R}-1}\}
denote the exponential family generated by
\bm{p}
with the corresponding parameters defined by
\bm{\gamma}
, i.e., for any
s\in\mathbb{S}_{R}
, the
s
-th component
p_{s}^{\bm{\gamma}}
of
\bm{p}^{\bm{\gamma}}
is given by

p_{s}^{\bm{\gamma}}=\frac{p_{s}\,e^{\gamma_{s}}}{p_{S_{R}}+\sum_{s=1}^{S_{R}-1}p_{s}\,e^{\gamma_{s}}},

(177)

where
\gamma_{S_{R}}=0
by convention. Then, it follows that for any fixed probability distribution
\bm{p}
on
\mathbb{S}_{R}
, the mapping in (
177
) defines a bijection between
\mathbb{R}^{S_{R}-1}
and the space of probability distributions on
\mathbb{S}_{R}
. Given any probability distribution
p
on
\mathbb{S}_{R}
, let
\bm{\gamma}_{\bm{p}}
denote the value of
\bm{\gamma}\in\mathbb{R}^{m-1}
such that
\bm{p}^{\bm{\gamma}_{\bm{p}}}=\bm{p}
. For later use, we record the below result from
[
42
]
specialized to our setting.

Lemma J.1
.

[
42
, Lemma 3]

For any two probability distributions
\bm{p},\bm{p}^{\prime}
on
\mathbb{S}_{R}
and
\bm{\gamma}\in\mathbb{R}^{S_{R}-1}
,

\langle\bm{\gamma},\bm{p}^{\prime}\rangle-\phi_{\bm{p}}(\bm{\gamma})=D_{\text{\rm KL}}(\bm{p}^{\prime}\|\bm{p})-D_{\text{\rm KL}}(\bm{p}^{\prime}\|\bm{p}^{\bm{\gamma}}),

(178)

where
p^{\bm{\gamma}}
denotes the member of the exponential family generated by
\bm{p}
with parameters given by
\bm{\gamma}
, defined via (
177
).

Consider the following mixture of martingales obtained by choosing
\bm{p}
according to the Dirichlet distribution
\mathcal{D}(1,\ldots,1)
on the simplex of dimension
S_{R}-1
:

M_{n}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)=\int M_{n}^{\bm{\gamma}_{\bm{p}}}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\,\frac{\prod_{s=1}^{S_{R}}p_{s}}{(\prod_{s=1}^{S_{R}}\Gamma(1))/\Gamma(S_{R})}\,\mathrm{d}p_{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}p_{S_{R}},\quad n\geq K-1.

(179)

Clearly, because a convex combination of martingales is a martingale, the mixture in (
179
) defines a martingale. Also, in (
179
),
\Gamma(\cdot)
denotes the Gamma function with the property that
\Gamma(k)=(k-1)!
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
.

An almost sure lower bound on
M_{n}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)
:

We note that for each
(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in\mathbb{S}_{R}\times[K]
, almost surely,

\displaystyle M_{n}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)

\displaystyle=\int\exp\left(N(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\,\left[\langle\bm{\gamma}_{\bm{p}},\widehat{Q}_{n}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\rangle-\phi_{\bm{\theta},\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a}(\bm{\gamma})\right]\right)\,\frac{\prod_{s=1}^{S_{R}}p_{s}}{(\prod_{s=1}^{S_{R}}\Gamma(1))/\Gamma(S_{R})}\,\mathrm{d}p_{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}p_{S_{R}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}\int\exp\left(N(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\,\left[D_{\text{\rm KL}}(\widehat{Q}_{n}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\|Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a))-D_{\text{\rm KL}}(\widehat{Q}_{n}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\|\bm{p})\right]\right)\,(S_{R}-1)!\,\mathrm{d}p_{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}p_{S_{R}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}\exp\left(N(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\,\left[D_{\text{\rm KL}}(\widehat{Q}_{n}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\|Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a))+H(\widehat{Q}_{n}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a))\right]\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 199.16928pt\cdot(S_{R}-1)!\cdot\int\prod_{s\in\mathbb{S}_{R}}p_{s}^{1+N(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\,\widehat{Q}_{n}(s|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)}\mathrm{d}p_{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}p_{S_{R}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{=}}\exp\left(N(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\,\left[D_{\text{\rm KL}}(\widehat{Q}_{n}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\|Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a))+H(\widehat{Q}_{n}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a))\right]\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 199.16928pt\cdot\frac{1}{\binom{N(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)}{N(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\,\widehat{Q}_{n}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)}}\cdot\frac{1}{\binom{N(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)+S_{R}-1}{S_{R}-1}},

(180)

where in the above set of equalities,
(a)
follows from the application of Lemma
J.1
to
\bm{p}=Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)
,
\bm{\gamma}=\bm{\gamma}_{\bm{p}}
, and
\bm{p}^{\prime}=\widehat{Q}_{n}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)
,
H(\cdot)
in
(b)
denotes the Shannon entropy of the argument probability distribution, and in
(c)
, the notation
\binom{N}{\bm{x}}
for any integer
N\in\mathbb{N}
and a vector
\bm{x}=[x_{1},\ldots,x_{N}]
with
\sum_{i=1}^{N}x_{i}=N
denotes the quantity

\binom{N}{\bm{x}}=\frac{N!}{\prod_{i=1}^{N}x_{i}!},

whereas for any two integers
N,m\in\mathbb{N}
such that
N>m
, the notation
\binom{N}{m}
denotes the quantity

\binom{N}{m}=\frac{N!}{m!\times(N-m)!}.

We now use the below result to upper bound the binomial coefficients appearing in (
180
).

Lemma J.2
.

[
43
, Theorem 11.1.3]

For any integers
N,m\in\mathbb{N}
such that
N>m
and a non-negative vector
\bm{x}=[x_{1},\ldots,x_{N}]
such that
\sum_{i=1}^{N}x_{i}=N
,

\binom{N}{\bm{x}}=\frac{N!}{\prod_{i=1}^{N}x_{i}!}\leq\exp\left(N\,H(\bm{x}/N)\right),\quad\binom{N}{m}\leq\exp\left(N\,H(m/N)\right),

(181)

where
H(\bm{x}/N)
denotes the Shannon entropy of the discrete probability distribution
\bm{x}/N\coloneqq[x_{1}/N,\ldots,x_{N}/N]
, and
H(m/N)
denotes the Shannon entropy of the Bernoulli distribution with mean
m/N
.

Using Lemma
J.2
to upper bound the binomial coefficients in (
180
), and simplifying the resulting expression, we get

\displaystyle M_{n}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)

\displaystyle\geq\exp\bigg(N(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\,D_{\text{\rm KL}}(\widehat{Q}_{n}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\|Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a))

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt-(N(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)+S_{R}-1)H((S_{R}-1)/(N(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)+S_{R}-1))\bigg)

(182)

almost surely for every
(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in\mathbb{S}_{R}\times[K]
.

A product martingale and proof completion:

Taking product over all
(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in\mathbb{S}_{R}\times[K]
in (
182
), we have almost surely

\displaystyle M_{n}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\prod_{(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in\mathbb{S}_{R}\times[K]}M_{n}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)

\displaystyle\geq\exp\bigg(\sum_{(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in\mathbb{S}_{R}\times[K]}N(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\,D_{\text{\rm KL}}(\widehat{Q}_{n}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\|Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a))

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt-\sum_{(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in\mathbb{S}_{R}\times[K]}(N(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)+S_{R}-1)H((S_{R}-1)/(N(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)+S_{R}-1))\bigg).

(183)

We now prove that
\{M_{n}\}_{n=K-1}^{\infty}
is a martingale and note that for each
(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in\mathbb{S}_{R}\times[K]
,

\displaystyle(N(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)+S_{R}-1)H((S_{R}-1)/(N(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)+S_{R}-1))

\displaystyle=(S_{R}-1)\,\log\left(1+\frac{N(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)}{S_{R}-1}\right)+N(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\,\log\left(1+\frac{S_{R}-1}{N(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}(S_{R}-1)\,\log\left(1+\frac{N(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)}{S_{R}-1}\right)+(S_{R}-1)

\displaystyle=(S_{R}-1)\,\log\left(e\left[1+\frac{N(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)}{S_{R}-1}\right]\right),

(184)

where
(a)
above follows from the inequality
\log(1+x)\leq x
. The fact that
\{M_{n}\}_{n=K-1}^{\infty}
is a martingale (with respect to the filtration in (
9
)) follows by noting that for any
(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in\mathbb{S}_{R}\times[K]
, using the shorthand notation
z_{n}=(\mathbf{d}(n),\mathbf{i}(n),A_{n})
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}}[M_{n}\mid\mathcal{F}_{n-1},z_{n}=(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}}\bigg[M_{n}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\times\prod_{(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime},a^{\prime})\neq(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)}M_{n}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime},a^{\prime})~\bigg|~\mathcal{F}_{n-1},\ z_{n}=(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}}\bigg[M_{n}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\times\prod_{(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime},a^{\prime})\neq(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)}M_{n-1}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime},a^{\prime})~\bigg|~\mathcal{F}_{n-1}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}}\bigg[M_{n}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\mid\mathcal{F}_{n-1}\bigg]\times\prod_{(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime},a^{\prime})\neq(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)}M_{n-1}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime},a^{\prime})

\displaystyle=M_{n-1}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\times\prod_{(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime},a^{\prime})\neq(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)}M_{n-1}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime},a^{\prime})

\displaystyle=M_{n-1},

(185)

where the third line follows by noting that conditioned on
\mathcal{F}_{n-1}
, the random variable
M_{n}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)
is independent of
\{M_{n-1}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime},a^{\prime}):(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime},a^{\prime})\neq(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\}
. Applying
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}}[\cdot]
on either side of (
185
) and using the tower property of conditional expectations, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}}[M_{n}]=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}}[\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}}[M_{n}\mid\mathcal{F}_{n-1},z_{n}]\mid\mathcal{F}_{n-1}]=M_{n-1}.

(186)

Noting that
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}}[M_{n}]=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}}[M_{K-1}]=1
for all
n\geq K-1
, and using Doob’s maximal inequality, we get

\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}\left(\exists n\geq K-1:M_{n}>\frac{1}{\delta}\right)\leq\delta\,\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}}[M_{K-1}]=\delta.

(187)

Clearly, (
187
) implies (
171
), thus proving the desired result.

Appendix K
Proof of Proposition
6.5

Fix
\delta\in(0,1)
and
\eta\in(0,1)
. Fix an arbitrary
\nu\in\mathcal{W}^{\star}(\bm{\theta})
, and let
\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star}
be as defined in Lemma
6.3
. Consider the event

\mathcal{E}=\left\{\lim_{n\to\infty}\max_{(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)}\bigg\lvert\frac{N(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)}{n-K+1}-\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\bigg\rvert=0,\quad\lim_{n\to\infty}\|\bm{\eta}(n)-\bm{\eta}\|_{\infty}=0\right\}.

(188)

Thanks to Lemma
6.2
, Lemma
6.3
, and the Borel–Cantelli lemma, we have
\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathcal{E})=1
under the non-stopping version of
\pi^{\textsc{Rstl-Dtrack}}
(with the same parameters as
\pi^{\textsc{Rstl-Dtrack}}
). Fix
\omega\in\mathcal{E}
and
\gamma>0
arbitrarily. Then, there exists
N_{\gamma}(\omega)\in\mathbb{N}
independent of
\delta
such that the following hold for all
n\geq N_{\gamma}(\omega)
:

\displaystyle\frac{Z(n,\omega)}{n}

\displaystyle\geq(1-\gamma)(\eta\,T_{\text{\rm unif}}^{\star}(\bm{\theta})+(1-\eta)\,T_{R}^{\star}(\bm{\theta})),

(189)

\displaystyle\zeta(n,\delta)

\displaystyle\leq\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta}\right)+\gamma\,(\eta\,T_{\text{\rm unif}}^{\star}(\bm{\theta})+(1-\eta)\,T_{R}^{\star}(\bm{\theta}))\,n.

(190)

The inequality in (
190
) follows by noting that
\zeta(n,\delta)=\log(1/\delta)+O(\log(n))=\log(1/\delta)+o(n)
. We then have

\displaystyle\tau(\omega)

\displaystyle=\inf\{n\geq K:Z(n,\omega)\geq\zeta(n,\delta)\}

\displaystyle\leq\inf\{n\geq N_{\gamma}(\omega):Z(n,\omega)\geq\zeta(n,\delta)\}

\displaystyle\leq\inf\left\{n\geq N_{\gamma}(\omega):n\,(1-\gamma)(\eta\,T_{\text{\rm unif}}^{\star}(\bm{\theta})+(1-\eta)\,T_{R}^{\star}(\bm{\theta}))\geq\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta}\right)+\gamma\,(\eta\,T_{\text{\rm unif}}^{\star}(\bm{\theta})+(1-\eta)\,T_{R}^{\star}(\bm{\theta}))\,n\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\inf\left\{n\geq N_{\gamma}(\omega):n\,(1-2\gamma)(\eta\,T_{\text{\rm unif}}^{\star}(\bm{\theta})+(1-\eta)\,T_{R}^{\star}(\bm{\theta}))\geq\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=\max\left\{N_{\gamma}(\omega),\quad\inf\left\{n\geq 1:n\,(1-2\gamma)(\eta\,T_{\text{\rm unif}}^{\star}(\bm{\theta})+(1-\eta)\,T_{R}^{\star}(\bm{\theta}))\geq\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta}\right)\right\}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\max\left\{N_{\gamma}(\omega),\quad\left\lceil\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{(1-2\gamma)(\eta\,T_{\text{\rm unif}}^{\star}(\bm{\theta})+(1-\eta)\,T_{R}^{\star}(\bm{\theta}))}\right\rceil\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\max\left\{N_{\gamma}(\omega),\quad 1+\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{(1-2\gamma)(\eta\,T_{\text{\rm unif}}^{\star}(\bm{\theta})+(1-\eta)\,T_{R}^{\star}(\bm{\theta}))}\right\}

(191)

for all
\omega\in\mathcal{E}
, where in writing the last line above, we use the relation
\lceil x\rceil<1+x
. Thus, it follows from (
191
) that
\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\tau<+\infty)=1
under
\pi^{\textsc{Rstl-Dtrack}}
, which in turn implies from (
49
) that
\pi^{\textsc{Rstl-Dtrack}}\in\Pi_{R}(\delta)
. Also, dividing both sides of (
191
) by
\log(1/\delta)
, letting
\delta\downarrow 0
and noting that
N_{\gamma}(\omega)
does not depend on
\delta
, we get

\limsup_{\delta\downarrow 0}\frac{\tau(\omega)}{\log(1/\delta)}\leq\frac{1}{(1-2\gamma)(\eta\,T_{\text{\rm unif}}^{\star}(\bm{\theta})+(1-\eta)\,T_{R}^{\star}(\bm{\theta}))}\quad\forall\omega\in\mathcal{E}.

(192)

Noting that the right-hand side of (
192
) holds for all
\gamma>0
, we arrive at (
50
) by taking limits as
\gamma\downarrow 0
.

Appendix L
Proof of Proposition
6.6

Notations:
We first introduce some notations. Let
\|\cdot\|_{1}
,
\|\cdot\|_{2}
, and
\|\cdot\|_{\infty}
denote the vector
1
-norm,
2
-norm, and sup-norm operators respectively. Further, for matrices
Q,Q^{\prime}
of identical dimensions, let

\|Q-Q^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\coloneqq\max_{(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)}\|Q(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)-Q^{\prime}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\|_{1}.

For all
n\geq K
, let
\bm{N}(n)\coloneqq[N(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a):(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in\mathbb{S}_{R}\times[K]]^{\top}
. Let
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{n}
denote the MDP with transition kernel
\widehat{Q}_{n}
defined in (
37
), and for any
\bm{\theta}\in\Theta^{K}
, let
\|\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{n}-\mathcal{M}_{\bm{\theta},R}\|_{\infty}
be as defined in (
221
).
Given
\bm{\theta}\in\Theta^{K}
, let
\bm{\eta}_{\bm{\theta}}\coloneqq[\eta_{\theta_{a}}:a\in[K]]^{\top}
. Also, let
\widehat{\bm{\eta}}(n)=[\widehat{\eta}_{a}(n):a\in[K]]^{\top}
,
\widehat{\theta}_{a}(n)=\dot{A}^{-1}(\widehat{\eta}_{a})
for all
a\in[K]
, and
\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(n)=[\widehat{\theta}_{a}(n):a\in[K]]^{\top}
.
For any
\bm{\theta}\in\Theta^{K}
and
\nu\in\mathcal{W}^{\star}(\bm{\theta})
, let
\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star}=[\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a):(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in\mathbb{S}_{R}\times[K]]^{\top}
be as defined in Lemma
6.3
. Let

\psi^{\prime}(\bm{\theta}^{\prime},\nu^{\prime},Q^{\prime})\coloneqq\inf_{\bm{\lambda}\in\textsc{Alt}(\bm{\theta}^{\prime})}\ \sum_{(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i})\in\mathbb{S}_{R}}\ \sum_{a=1}^{K}\nu^{\prime}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\,D_{\text{\rm KL}}(Q^{\prime}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\|Q_{\bm{\lambda},R}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)).

(193)

Proof of Proposition
6.6
.

Fix
\bm{\theta}\in\Theta^{K}
,
\eta\in(0,1)
,
\nu\in\mathcal{W}^{\star}(\bm{\theta})
, and
\delta\in(0,1)
. Assume that
\bm{\theta}
is the underlying instance. Fix an arbitrary
\xi>0
. From Lemma
J.1
, we know that the mapping
\bm{\theta}^{\prime}\mapsto\bm{\eta}_{\bm{\theta}^{\prime}}
is continuous. Hence, there exists
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(\xi)\leq\xi
such that

\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{\prime}\|_{2}<\varepsilon\implies\|\bm{\eta}_{\bm{\theta}}-\bm{\eta}_{\bm{\theta}^{\prime}}\|_{2}<\xi.

(194)

Given
N\geq K
, let
8
8

8

We assume, without loss of generality, that
N^{3/4}
is an integer.

C_{N}^{3}(\xi)\coloneqq\bigcap_{n=N^{5}}^{N^{6}}\bigg\{\bigg\|\frac{\bm{N}(n)}{n-K+1}-\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star}\bigg\|_{\infty}\leq K_{\xi}(\bm{\theta},\nu)\,\xi\bigg\},

(195)

where
K_{\xi}(\bm{\theta},\nu)
are as defined in Lemma
6.3
. Let
C_{N}^{1}(\xi)
and
C_{N}^{2}(\xi)
be as defined in (
222
) and (
44
) respectively. Let

\psi^{\star}(\bm{\theta},\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star},\xi)\coloneqq\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{\theta}^{\prime}:\|\bm{\theta}^{\prime}-\bm{\theta}\|_{2}\leq\varepsilon\\
\nu^{\prime}:\|\nu^{\prime}-\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star}\|_{\infty}\leq K_{\xi}\,\xi\\
Q^{\prime}:\|Q-Q_{\bm{\theta},R}\|_{\infty}\leq\xi\end{subarray}}\psi^{\prime}(\theta^{\prime},\nu^{\prime},Q^{\prime}),

(196)

Notice that as a consequence of (
41
), the following convergences hold almost surely:

\|\widehat{\bm{\eta}}(n)-\bm{\eta}\|_{2}\to 0,\quad\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(n)-\bm{\theta}\|_{2}\to 0,\quad\|\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{n}-\mathcal{M}_{\bm{\theta},R}\|_{\infty}\to 0.

(197)

Therefore, there exists
N_{1}=N_{1}(\xi)\geq K
such that for all
N\geq N_{1}
, the following hold:

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(n)-\bm{\theta}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon,\qquad N^{5}\leq n\leq N^{6},

(198)

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\bm{\eta}}(n)-\bm{\eta}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\xi,\qquad N^{5}\leq n\leq N^{6},

(199)

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{n}-\mathcal{M}_{\bm{\theta},R}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\xi,\qquad N^{5}\leq n\leq N^{6},

(200)

\displaystyle\psi\left(\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(n),\frac{\bm{N}(n)}{n-K+1}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\psi^{\star}(\bm{\theta},\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star},\xi),\qquad N^{5}\leq n\leq N^{6}.

(201)

Furthermore, it follows from Lemma
M.3
and Lemma
6.2
that
\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}}[N_{1}(\xi)]<+\infty
(this is simply an artefact of the probability term
\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(N_{1}>n)
decaying exponentially in
n
). Hence, it follows that for all
N\geq N_{1}
, the event
C_{N}^{3}(\xi)
holds with high probability, conditioned on
C_{N}^{1}(\xi)
. Furthermore, noting that
N_{a}(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\leq n+1
and therefore
\zeta(n,\delta)=\log(1/\delta)+O(\log n)
, it follows that there exists
N_{2}=N_{2}(\xi)\geq K
such that

\zeta(n,\delta)\leq\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta}\right)+\xi\,\psi^{\star}(\bm{\theta},\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star},\xi)\,n\qquad\forall n\geq N_{2}.

(202)

Let
N_{3}=N_{3}(\xi,\delta)
be defined as

N_{3}(\xi,\delta)=\inf\left\{n\geq K:n-K+1\geq\frac{1}{(1-\xi)\,\psi^{\star}(\bm{\theta},\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star},\xi)}\,\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta}\right)\right\}.

(203)

Then, for all
N\geq\max\{N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}\}
, it follows that conditional on
C_{N}^{1}(\xi)\cap C_{N}^{2}(\xi)\cap C_{N}^{3}(\xi)
,

\displaystyle Z(N)

\displaystyle=\inf_{\bm{\lambda}\in\textsc{Alt}(\bm{\theta}(N))}\sum_{(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i})\in\mathbb{S}_{R}}\ \sum_{a=1}^{K}N(N,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\,D_{\text{\rm KL}}(\widehat{Q}_{N}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\|Q_{\bm{\lambda},R}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a))

\displaystyle\geq(N-K+1)\,\psi^{\star}(\bm{\theta},\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star},\xi)

\displaystyle\geq(N-K+1)\,\xi\,\psi^{\star}(\bm{\theta},\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star},\xi)+\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\zeta(N,\delta),

thereby proving that

C_{N}^{1}(\xi)\cap C_{N}^{2}(\xi)\cap C_{N}^{3}(\xi)\subset\{\tau\leq N\}\qquad\forall N\geq\max\{N_{1}(\xi),N_{2}(\xi),N_{3}(\xi,\delta)\}.

(204)

Noting that
N_{1}(\xi)
is a random variable, while
N_{2}(\xi),N_{3}(\xi,\delta)
are deterministic constants, for any
n\geq K
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}}[\tau\,\mathbf{1}_{\{N_{1}(\xi)=n\}}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{N=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\tau>N,\,N_{1}(\xi)=n)

\displaystyle\leq\max\{n,N_{2}(\xi),N_{3}(\xi,\delta)\}+\sum_{N=\max\{n,N_{2},N_{3}\}+1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\tau>N)

\displaystyle\leq\max\{n,N_{2}(\xi),N_{3}(\xi,\delta)\}+\sum_{N=\max\{N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}\}+1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}\bigg(\overline{C_{N}^{1}(\xi)}\cup\overline{C_{N}^{2}(\xi)}\cup\overline{C_{N}^{3}(\xi)}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\max\{n,N_{2}(\xi),N_{3}(\xi,\delta)\}+\sum_{N=\max\{N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}\}+1}^{\infty}\bigg[\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}\bigg(\overline{C_{N}^{1}(\xi)}\bigg)+\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}\bigg(\overline{C_{N}^{2}(\xi)}\bigg)+\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}\bigg(\overline{C_{N}^{3}(\xi)}~\bigg|~C_{N}^{2}(\xi)\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\max\{n,N_{2}(\xi),N_{3}(\xi,\delta)\}+\sum_{N=1}^{\infty}\bigg[\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}\bigg(\overline{C_{N}^{1}(\xi)}\bigg)+\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}\bigg(\overline{C_{N}^{2}(\xi)}\bigg)+\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}\bigg(\overline{C_{N}^{3}(\xi)}~\bigg|~C_{N}^{2}(\xi)\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq n+N_{2}(\xi)+N_{3}(\xi,\delta)+\sum_{N=1}^{\infty}\bigg[\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}\bigg(\overline{C_{N}^{1}(\xi)}\bigg)+\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}\bigg(\overline{C_{N}^{2}(\xi)}\bigg)+\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}\bigg(\overline{C_{N}^{3}(\xi)}~\bigg|~C_{N}^{2}(\xi)\bigg)\bigg],

(205)

from which it follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}}[\tau]

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}}[\tau_{\delta}\,\mathbf{1}_{\{N_{1}(\xi)=n\}}]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}}[N_{1}(\xi)]+N_{2}(\xi)+N_{3}(\xi,\delta)+\sum_{N=1}^{\infty}\bigg[\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}\bigg(\overline{C_{N}^{1}(\xi)}\bigg)+\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}\bigg(\overline{C_{N}^{2}(\xi)}\bigg)+\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}\bigg(\overline{C_{N}^{3}(\xi)}~\bigg|~C_{N}^{2}(\xi)\bigg)\bigg].

(206)

From Lemma
M.3
, Lemma
6.2
, and Lemma
6.3
, we know that the infinite-summation term in (
206
) is finite. Dividing both sides of (
206
) by
\log(1/\delta)
and taking limits as
\delta\downarrow 0
, we get

\displaystyle\limsup_{\delta\downarrow 0}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}}[\tau_{\pi}]}{\log(1/\delta)}

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{\delta\downarrow 0}\frac{N_{3}(\xi,\delta)}{\log(1/\delta)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(1-\xi)\,\psi^{\star}(\bm{\theta},\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star},\xi)}.

(207)

Taking limits as
\xi\downarrow 0
, using the fact that
\limsup_{\xi\downarrow 0}K_{\xi}(\bm{\theta},\nu)<+\infty
(cf. Lemma
6.3
), and noting that

\lim_{\xi\downarrow 0}\psi^{\star}(\bm{\theta},\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star},\xi)=\psi(\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star},\bm{\theta}),

(208)

where
\psi
is as defined in (
30
), we get

\limsup_{\delta\downarrow 0}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}}[\tau_{\pi}]}{\log(1/\delta)}\leq\frac{1}{\psi(\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star},\bm{\theta})}.

(209)

Noting that

\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star}=\eta\,\nu_{\bm{\theta}}^{\text{\rm unif}}+(1-\eta)\,\nu,

and that

\displaystyle\psi(\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star},\bm{\theta})

\displaystyle=\inf_{\bm{\lambda}\in\textsc{Alt}(\bm{\theta})}\sum_{(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i})\in\mathbb{S}_{R}}\ \sum_{a=1}^{K}\omega_{\bm{\theta},\nu}^{\star}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\,D_{\text{\rm KL}}(Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\|Q_{\bm{\lambda},R}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a))

\displaystyle=\inf_{\bm{\lambda}\in\textsc{Alt}(\bm{\theta})}\sum_{(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i})\in\mathbb{S}_{R}}\ \sum_{a=1}^{K}(\eta\,\nu_{\bm{\theta}}^{\text{\rm unif}}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)+(1-\eta)\,\nu(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a))\,D_{\text{\rm KL}}(Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\|Q_{\bm{\lambda},R}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a))

\displaystyle\geq\eta\,T_{\text{\rm unif}}^{\star}(\bm{\theta})+(1-\eta)\,T_{R}^{\star}(\bm{\theta}),

where
T_{R}^{\star}(\bm{\theta})
appears because
\nu\in\mathcal{W}^{\star}(\bm{\theta})
,
we get

\limsup_{\delta\downarrow 0}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\theta}}[\tau_{\pi}]}{\log(1/\delta)}\leq\frac{1}{\eta\,T_{\text{\rm unif}}^{\star}(\bm{\theta})+(1-\eta)\,T_{R}^{\star}(\bm{\theta})}.

(210)

Finally, taking limits as
\eta\downarrow 0
in (
210
) yields (
53
).
∎

Appendix M
Technical Results

In this section, we record some technical results of interest. First, we introduce some notations. Fix
\bm{\theta}\in\Theta^{K}
. For any
n\geq K
, let
Q_{n}\coloneqq Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{n}}
denote the transition kernel of the MDP
\mathcal{M}_{\bm{\theta},R}
under
\pi_{n}
.
Along similar lines as in the proof of Lemma
3.3
, it is easy to show that implies that
Q_{n}
is ergodic for each
n\geq K
. Let
\omega_{n}^{\star}=[\omega_{n}^{\star}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a):(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in\mathbb{S}_{R}\times[K]]^{\top}
denote the unique stationary distribution of
Q_{n}
. Let
W_{n}=\mathbf{1}\omega_{n}^{T}
denote the rank-
1
matrix each of whose rows is equal to
\omega_{n}^{T}
. For
r\in\mathbb{N}
, let
Q_{n}^{r}
denote the
r
-fold self-product of
Q_{n}
. Let
\|Q_{n}^{r}-W_{n}\|_{\infty}=\max_{(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)}\|Q_{n}^{r}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)-W_{n}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\|_{1}
, where
\|\cdot\|_{1}
denotes vector
1
-norm.

Let
\omega_{\bm{\theta}}^{\text{\rm unif}}=[\omega_{\bm{\theta}}^{\text{\rm unif}}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a):(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in\mathbb{S}_{R}\times[K]]^{\top}
denote the unique stationary distribution of the MDP
\mathcal{M}_{\bm{\theta},R}
under
\pi^{\text{\rm unif}}
. Let
\mathbb{V}
be as defined in Lemma
6.1
. Thanks to Lemma
3.3
, the following quantities are well-defined:

\displaystyle r_{\bm{\theta},\text{\rm unif}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\min\left\{r\in\mathbb{N}:\forall(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a),(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime},a^{\prime})\in\mathbb{V},\quad Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi^{\text{\rm unif}}}^{r}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime},a^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)>0\right\},

(211)

\displaystyle\sigma_{\bm{\theta},\text{\rm unif}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\min_{(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a),(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime},a^{\prime})\in\mathbb{V}}\frac{Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi^{\text{\rm unif}}}^{r_{\bm{\theta},\text{\rm unif}}}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime},a^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)}{\omega_{\bm{\theta}}^{\text{\rm unif}}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime},a^{\prime})}.

(212)

Given a policy
\pi=[\pi(a|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i}):(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in\mathbb{S}_{R}\times[K]]^{\top}
, let
U_{\pi}\coloneqq\{(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a):\pi(a|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i})>0\}
. Let
U_{n}\coloneqq U_{\pi_{n}^{\eta}}
,
n\geq K
.

M.1
A Bound on
\|Q_{n}^{r}-W_{n}\|_{\infty}
for
r\in\mathbb{N}

The below result from
[
37
]
gives a bound on the rate of convergence of powers of an ergodic transition kernel to the stationary distribution.

Lemma M.1
.

[
37
, Theorem 4.9]

Let
Q
be an ergodic stochastic matrix on a finite state space
\mathcal{Z}
with stationary distribution
\omega
. Let
W
denote the rank-
1
matrix each of whose rows is equal to
\omega^{\top}
. Suppose that there exists
\sigma>0
and
r_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
Q^{r_{0}}(z,z^{\prime})\geq\sigma\omega(z^{\prime})
for all
z,z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}
. Then,

\|Q^{r}-W\|_{\infty}\leq 2(1-\sigma)^{r/r_{0}-1}\quad\forall r\in\mathbb{N}.

(213)

We then have the below result for the transition kernel
Q_{n}^{r}
.

Lemma M.2
.

Fix
\bm{\theta}\in\Theta^{K}
. For any
n\geq K
, let
\pi_{n}^{\eta}\coloneqq\pi_{\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(n)}^{\eta}
. Let

\displaystyle\sigma(\varepsilon,\pi,\omega)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left(\varepsilon^{r_{\bm{\theta},\text{\rm unif}}}+\left((1-\varepsilon)\,K\min_{(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in U_{\pi}}\pi(a|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i})\right)^{r_{\bm{\theta},\text{\rm unif}}}\,\right)\cdot\sigma_{\bm{\theta},\text{\rm unif}}\cdot\left(\min_{(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)}\frac{\omega_{\bm{\theta},\text{\rm unif}}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)}{\omega(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)}\right),

(214)

\displaystyle\bar{\sigma}(\varepsilon,\pi,\omega)

\displaystyle\coloneqq 1-\sigma(\varepsilon,\pi,\omega),.

(215)

Then, we have

\|Q_{n}^{r}-W_{n}\|_{\infty}\leq C_{n}\,\rho_{n}^{r}\quad\forall r\in\mathbb{N},

(216)

where
C_{n}=2/\bar{\sigma}(\varepsilon_{n},\pi_{n-1}^{\eta},\omega_{n})
and
\rho_{n}=\bar{\sigma}(\varepsilon_{n},\pi_{n-1}^{\eta},\omega_{n})^{1/r_{\bm{\theta},\text{\rm unif}}}
.

Proof.

Recall that

Q_{n}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime},a^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)=\varepsilon_{n}\,Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi^{\text{\rm unif}}}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime},a^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)+(1-\varepsilon_{n})\,Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{n-1}^{\eta}}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime},a^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a).

(217)

We note that for all
(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a),(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime},a^{\prime})\in\mathbb{S}_{R}\times[K]
,

\displaystyle Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{n-1}^{\eta}}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime},a^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)

\displaystyle=Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\cdot\pi_{n-1}^{\eta}(a^{\prime}|\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\cdot K\,\pi_{n-1}^{\eta}(a^{\prime}|\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime})\cdot\frac{1}{K}

\displaystyle\geq Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\cdot K\,\min_{(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in U_{n-1}}\pi_{n-1}^{\eta}(a|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i})\cdot\pi^{\text{\rm unif}}(a^{\prime}|\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=K\,\min_{(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in U_{n-1}}\pi_{n-1}^{\eta}(a|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i})\cdot Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi^{\text{\rm unif}}}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime},a^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a),

(218)

where in writing the inequality above, we make use of the relation

\pi_{n-1}^{\eta}(a^{\prime}|\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime})\cdot\frac{1}{K}\geq\min_{(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in U_{n-1}}\pi_{n-1}^{\eta}(a|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i})\cdot\pi^{\text{\rm unif}}(a^{\prime}|\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime})\quad\forall(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime},a^{\prime}).

(219)

We then note that for all
(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a),(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime},a^{\prime})\in\mathbb{V}
,

\displaystyle Q_{n}^{r_{\bm{\theta},\text{\rm unif}}}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime},a^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)

\displaystyle\geq\varepsilon_{n}^{r_{\bm{\theta}},\text{\rm unif}}\,Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi^{\text{\rm unif}}}^{r_{\bm{\theta},\text{\rm unif}}}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime},a^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)+(1-\varepsilon_{n})^{r_{\bm{\theta},\text{\rm unif}}}\,Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi_{n-1}^{\eta}}^{r_{\bm{\theta},\text{\rm unif}}}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime},a^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\geq}}\left(\varepsilon_{n}^{r_{\bm{\theta}},\text{\rm unif}}+\left((1-\varepsilon_{n})\,K\,\min_{(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in U_{n-1}}\pi_{n-1}^{\eta}(a|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i})\right)^{r_{\bm{\theta},\text{\rm unif}}}\,\right)\,Q_{\bm{\theta},\pi^{\text{\rm unif}}}^{r_{\bm{\theta},\text{\rm unif}}}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime},a^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\geq}}\left(\varepsilon_{n}^{r_{\bm{\theta}},\text{\rm unif}}+\left((1-\varepsilon_{n})\,K\,\min_{(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in U_{n-1}}\pi_{n-1}^{\eta}(a|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i})\right)^{r_{\bm{\theta},\text{\rm unif}}}\,\right)\cdot\sigma_{\bm{\theta},\text{\rm unif}}\cdot\omega_{\bm{\theta},\text{\rm unif}}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime},a^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\left(\varepsilon_{n}^{r_{\bm{\theta}},\text{\rm unif}}+\left((1-\varepsilon_{n})\,K\,\min_{(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in U_{n-1}}\pi_{n-1}^{\eta}(a|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i})\right)^{r_{\bm{\theta},\text{\rm unif}}}\,\right)\cdot\sigma_{\bm{\theta},\text{\rm unif}}\cdot\frac{\omega_{\bm{\theta},\text{\rm unif}}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime},a^{\prime})}{\omega_{n}^{\star}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime},a^{\prime})}\cdot\omega_{n}^{\star}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime},a^{\prime})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{\geq}}\underbrace{\left(\varepsilon_{n}^{r_{\bm{\theta}},\text{\rm unif}}+\left((1-\varepsilon_{n})\,K\,\min_{(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in U_{n-1}}\pi_{n-1}^{\eta}(a|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i})\right)^{r_{\bm{\theta},\text{\rm unif}}}\,\right)\cdot\sigma_{\bm{\theta},\text{\rm unif}}\cdot\left(\min_{(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in\mathbb{V}}\frac{\omega_{\bm{\theta},\text{\rm unif}}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)}{\omega_{n}^{\star}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)}\right)}_{=\sigma(\varepsilon_{n},\pi_{n-1}^{\eta},\omega_{n}^{\star})}\cdot\,\omega_{n}^{\star}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime},a^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\sigma(\varepsilon_{n},\pi_{n-1}^{\eta},\omega_{n}^{\star})\cdot\omega_{n}^{\star}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime},a^{\prime}),

(220)

where
(a)
above follows from an application of (
218
) for a total of
r_{\bm{\theta},\text{\rm unif}}
times in succession, and
(b)
follows from (
212
). Noting that (
220
) holds for all
(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a),(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime},a^{\prime})\in\mathbb{V}
, a simple application of Lemma
M.1
with
\sigma=\sigma(\varepsilon_{n},\pi_{n-1}^{\eta},\omega_{n}^{\star})
and
r_{0}=r_{\bm{\theta},\text{\rm unif}}
yields the desired result.
∎

M.2
Concentration of Empirical Transition Kernel

In this section, we record a result on the concentration of the empirical transition kernel
\widehat{Q}_{n}
defined in (
37
) under a indefinitely running version of the policy
\pi^{\textsc{Rstl-Dtrack}}
(i.e., a policy that does not check for the stopping criterion and runs indefinitely by selecting arms according to (
36
)). We begin with some notations. For any
n\geq K
, let
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{n}
denote the MDP whose state space is
\mathbb{S}_{R}
, action space is
[K]
, and the transition kernel is specified by
\widehat{Q}_{n}
.
For any
\bm{\theta}\in\Theta^{K}
, let

\|\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{n}-\mathcal{M}_{\bm{\theta},R}\|_{\infty}\coloneqq\max_{(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)}\|\widehat{Q}_{n}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)-Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\|_{1},

(221)

where
\|\cdot\|_{1}
denotes the vector
1
-norm operator.
We then have the following result.

Lemma M.3
.

Fix
\bm{\theta}\in\Theta^{K}
and
\xi>0
. For
N\geq K
, let
9
9

9

We assume, without loss of generality, that
N^{1/4}
is an integer.

C_{N}^{1}(\xi)\coloneqq\bigcap_{n=N^{5}}^{N^{6}}\bigg\{\|\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{n}-\mathcal{M}_{\bm{\theta},R}\|_{\infty}\leq\xi\bigg\}.

(222)

Then, there exist constants
B
and
C
that depend only on
\xi
and
\bm{\theta}
such that under the indefinite version of
\pi^{\textsc{Rstl-Dtrack}}
,

\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}\bigg(\overline{C_{N}^{1}(\xi)}\bigg)\leq\frac{1}{N^{2}}+B\,N^{6}\,\exp\left(-\frac{C\,N^{1/4}}{\sqrt{K\,S_{R}}}\right)\quad\forall N\geq K.

(223)

Proof.

Consider the event

\mathcal{E}_{N}\coloneqq\bigg\{\forall(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in\mathbb{V},\quad\forall n\geq K,\quad N(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\geq\left\lceil\frac{n}{\lambda_{\bm{\theta}}(N)}\right\rceil^{1/4}-1\bigg\},

(224)

where
\lambda_{\bm{\theta}}(N)\coloneqq\frac{(1+S_{R})^{2}}{\sigma_{\bm{\theta}}^{2}}\log^{2}(1+KS_{R}\,N^{2})
, and
\mathbb{V}
is as defined in Lemma
6.1
. In the definition of
\lambda_{\bm{\theta}}(N)
, the constant
\sigma_{\bm{\theta}}
is as defined in Lemma
G.2
. From Lemma
6.1
, we know that
\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathcal{E}_{N})\geq 1-1/N^{2}
. We thus have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\overline{C_{N}^{1}(\xi)})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\overline{\mathcal{E}_{N}})+\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\overline{C_{N}^{1}(\xi)}\cap\mathcal{E}_{N})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N^{2}}+\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\overline{C_{N}^{1}(\xi)}\cap\mathcal{E}_{N}).

(225)

Using the union bound, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}(\overline{C_{N}^{1}(\xi)}\cap\mathcal{E}_{N})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n=N^{5}}^{N^{6}}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}\bigg(\exists(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in\mathbb{V}:\|\widehat{Q}_{n}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)-Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\cdot\mid\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\|_{1}>\xi,\ \mathcal{E}_{N}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n=N^{5}}^{N^{6}}\ \sum_{(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in\mathbb{V}}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}\bigg(\exists(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{S}_{R}:|\widehat{Q}_{n}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)-Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)|>\frac{\xi}{S_{R}},\ \mathcal{E}_{N}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n=N^{5}}^{N^{6}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in\mathbb{V}\\
(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{S}_{R}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}\bigg(|\widehat{Q}_{n}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)-Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)|>\frac{\xi}{S_{R}},\ \mathcal{E}_{N}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n=N^{5}}^{N^{6}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in\mathbb{V}\\
(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{S}_{R}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}\bigg(\widehat{Q}_{n}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)-Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)>\frac{\xi}{S_{R}},\ \mathcal{E}_{N}\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum_{n=N^{5}}^{N^{6}}\ \sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in\mathbb{V}\\
(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{S}_{R}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}\bigg(\widehat{Q}_{n}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)-Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)<-\frac{\xi}{S_{R}},\ \mathcal{E}_{N}\bigg).

(226)

For
x,y\in[0,1]
, let
d(x,y)\coloneqq x\log(x/y)+(1-x)\log((1-x)/(1-y))
. For all
(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in\mathbb{V}
,
(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{S}_{R}
, and
n\geq N^{5}
, we then have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}\bigg(\widehat{Q}_{n}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)-Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)>\frac{\xi}{S_{R}},\ \mathcal{E}_{N}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}\bigg(\widehat{Q}_{n}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)-Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)>\frac{\xi}{S_{R}},\ N(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\geq\left\lceil\frac{n}{\lambda_{\bm{\theta}}(N)}\right\rceil^{1/4}-1\bigg)

\displaystyle=\sum_{u=\left\lceil\frac{n}{\lambda_{\bm{\theta}}(N)}\right\rceil^{1/4}-1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}\bigg(\widehat{Q}_{n}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)-Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)>\frac{\xi}{S_{R}},\ N(n,\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)=u\bigg)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\sum_{u=\left\lceil\frac{n}{\lambda_{\bm{\theta}}(N)}\right\rceil^{1/4}-1}^{\infty}\exp\left(-u\cdot d\left(Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)+\frac{\xi}{S_{R}},Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{\exp\left(-\left(\left\lceil\frac{n}{\lambda_{\bm{\theta}}(N)}\right\rceil^{1/4}-1\right)\cdot d\left(Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)+\frac{\xi}{S_{R}},Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\right)\right)}{1-\exp\left(-d\left(Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)+\frac{\xi}{S_{R}},Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\right)\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{\exp\left(-\left(\frac{N^{5/4}}{\lambda_{\bm{\theta}}(N)^{1/4}}-1\right)\cdot d\left(Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)+\frac{\xi}{S_{R}},Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\right)\right)}{1-\exp\left(-d\left(Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime},|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)+\frac{\xi}{S_{R}},Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\right)\right)},

(227)

where
(a)
above follows from the Chernoff–Hoeffding bound. Along similar lines as above, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\bm{\theta}}\bigg(\widehat{Q}_{n}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)-Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)<-\frac{\xi}{S_{R}},\ \mathcal{E}_{N}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{\exp\left(-\left(\frac{N^{5/4}}{\lambda_{\bm{\theta}}(N)^{1/4}}-1\right)\cdot d\left(Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)-\frac{\xi}{S_{R}},Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\right)\right)}{1-\exp\left(-d\left(Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)-\frac{\xi}{S_{R}},Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime},|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\right)\right)}.

(228)

Plugging (
227
) and (
228
) into (
226
), setting

\displaystyle C

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sqrt{\frac{\sigma_{\bm{\theta}}}{1+S_{R}}}\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in\mathbb{V}\\
(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{S}_{R}\end{subarray}}\bigg\{d\left(Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)-\frac{\xi}{S_{R}},~~Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\right),

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378ptd\left(Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)+\frac{\xi}{S_{R}},~~Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\right)\bigg\},

(229)

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\in\mathbb{V}\\
(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{S}_{R}\end{subarray}}\bigg[\dfrac{\exp\left(d\left(Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)+\frac{\xi}{S_{R}},~~Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\right)\right)}{1-\exp\left(-d\left(Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)+\frac{\xi}{S_{R}},~~Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\right)\right)}

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt+\dfrac{\exp\left(d\left(Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)-\frac{\xi}{S_{R}},~~Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\right)\right)}{1-\exp\left(-d\left(Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)-\frac{\xi}{S_{R}},~~Q_{\bm{\theta},R}(\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}^{\prime}|\mathbf{d},\mathbf{i},a)\right)\right)}\bigg],

(230)

and
noting that

\displaystyle\sum_{n=N^{5}}^{N^{6}}\exp\left(-\frac{C\,N^{5/4}}{\sqrt{\log(1+KS_{R}\,N^{2})}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq N^{6}\exp\left(-\,\frac{C\,N^{5/4}}{\sqrt{\log(1+KS_{R}\,N^{2})}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq N^{6}\exp\left(-\,\frac{C\,N^{5/4}}{\sqrt{K\,S_{R}\,N^{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle=N^{6}\exp\left(-\,\frac{C\,N^{1/4}}{\sqrt{K\,S_{R}}}\right),

we arrive at (
223
).
∎
